## Executive Recommendation

- Keep one `FrameMgr.cpp`/`FrameMgr.h` module under `NexusTK/ui/core/`; do not create a `FrameScheduler.cpp`, custom LinkedList implementation, feature-owned copy, or raw helper file.
- Use period-compatible C++03 with `std::list<FrameScheduleEntry>`, natural x86 layout, no `auto`, `nullptr`, lambdas, raw-address casts, decompiler names, manual vptr writes, or hand-authored RTTI/destructor wrappers.
- Treat `AdvanceCallbackFrame` as the best descriptive retained-method spelling for `[0x004b7040,0x004b70a5)`. Its exact original spelling/access and current liveness are lost, so they cap confidence but do not justify dropping a complete ordinary member body.
- Preserve `g_frameRegistry` as the best established source spelling. Emit one extern in `FrameMgr.h`, one omitted-initializer definition in `FrameMgr.cpp`, and explicit `Singleton<FrameMgr>` specializations matching the accepted project pattern.

## Heuristic / Inference Reanalysis And Validation

| Issue | Reanalysis and result | Classification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Container identity | Sentinel pointer at `+0x10`, count at `+0x14`, 16-byte nodes, eight-byte payload, `list<T> too long`, and shared `StdList*` helpers prove old-Dinkumware `std::list<FrameScheduleEntry>`. | Very strong inference from direct lowering |
| Callback record | Node payload is exactly handler pointer at node `+8` and due frame at node `+12`; source record is eight bytes. | Direct fact |
| Context layout | `callbackFrame +0`, `currentFrame +4`, `dispatching +8` within a 12-byte context at manager `+4`; natural three-byte tail padding. | Direct fact |
| Return contract | Dispatch calls vtable slot `+4` and never tests EAX; ScreenPane has multiple valueless returns; SpelledPane and WorldMapPane return only incidental call/arithmetic residue. | Direct fact; current bool/int types rejected |
| Insert policy | The walk continues while new due frame is greater than or equal to the current due frame, then inserts before the first larger entry. | Direct stable-order fact |
| Delayed scheduling | Wrapper reads `callbackFrame`, not `currentFrame`, before adding delay. | Direct correction |
| Ordinary destructor | `[0x004b6e70,0x004b6e93)` is exact list/Singleton/LObject teardown and is produced by an implicit FrameMgr destructor. | Compiler-generated source consequence |
| Dormant drain | `[0x004b7040,0x004b70a5)` is a complete EBP-framed member, drains due entries, invokes callbacks, and increments callbackFrame despite zero start xrefs/pointer hits. | Retained source-authored method |
| Boost label | The body writes FrameHandler vtable state and unregisters through g_frameRegistry; `boost::exception` is type/name pollution. | Rejected |
| Feature ownership | ScreenPane, SpelledPane, WorldMapPane, and EventDispatcher consume scheduler interfaces but do not own queue policy/storage. | Rejected |
| Raw source | Manual list nodes, vtable arrays, scalar flags, explicit base destructor calls, EH/cookie code, and raw offsets are lowering, not source. | Rejected |

## Positive Evidence Summary

- The scheduler forms one coherent source unit: FrameHandler wrappers call one global FrameMgr, EventDispatcher advances it, and three proven derived classes implement the same virtual callback slot.
- RTTI proves `FrameMgr : LObject, Singleton<FrameMgr>` and exact EBO displacement `+4`; constructor/list/global behavior proves the complete 0x18 layout.
- All list algorithms map cleanly to standard `std::list` operations and an eight-byte source record. No custom container source is needed.
- Both previously raw islands now have exact source/compiler dispositions, removing the prior file-level blank-source blocker.
- The source draft has one definition for every authored entity and delegates all compiler/library artifacts to the toolchain.

| ---: | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | UID0000JM FrameMgr | Queue policy, FrameHandler interface, FrameMgr class, global, singleton lifecycle, direct generated route. | Canonical file owner |
| 2 | UID00005G/UID00005H | Exact semantic class owners within the file. | Source emitters |
| 3 | EventDispatcher/Application | Constructs, destroys, and ticks manager. | Consumer/lifetime driver only |
| 4 | ScreenPane/SpelledPane/WorldMapPane | Implement callback and invoke wrappers. | Derived consumers only |
| 5 | LinkedList/std library | Supplies generic node algorithms. | Library support, not source owner |
| 6 | FramePartPane/FrameChrome/TimerMgr | Excluded adjacent UI factory ownership, historical source grouping, and separate millisecond timer scheduler. | Rejected |
| 7 | Boost exception | Polluted name/type at FrameHandler destructor. | Rejected |

## Source Placement

- Final path remains `NexusTK/ui/core/FrameMgr.cpp` with companion `FrameMgr.h`.
- FrameMgr.h is the canonical declaration site for FrameUpdateContext because all callback implementers consume it and the scheduler owns its fields.
- EventDispatcher.cpp includes FrameMgr.h for the complete member-call type. Derived class sources include FrameMgr.h through their own declaration routes.
- No standalone `FrameScheduler`, `FrameUpdateContext`, `FrameScheduleEntry`, or custom list file is warranted.

## Range / Split / Padding / Reclassification Analysis

- Keep UID00015M exactly `[0x004b6cb0,0x004b7112)`. Correct the dormant helper end from stale `0x004b70a3` to exact exclusive `0x004b70a5`; final `retn 4` is `[0x004b70a2,0x004b70a5)`.

- No third-party import, binary blob, generated-file edit, or IDA patch is required.

P02.15 is deliberately a no-change protection, not a new type action. Fresh lookup, profile, entry-item, decompile, and xref reads agree that the dated Gate 2B evidence database models `sub_4B7120` as `void *()` and leaves the fifth allocation/constructor result in EAX. Current UID00022F documentation separately reconstructs the human-written helper as `void BuildFramePartPanes()` under UID000578 because all useful behavior is its five global slot writes and no caller is known to consume a return. Those statements describe different layers: P02.15 must preserve the literal IDA prestate, while formal source may suppress incidental return-register residue. Changing the excluded successor type during UID0000JM would cross ownership, contradict the zero-xref/no-consumer evidence, and expand the accepted 39-action handoff without a FrameMgr need; therefore the correct executable repair is `void *()` no-change and the action/protection totals remain exactly 39/60.

The A/P tables are structured entity contracts, not a supervisor execution or persistence program. Row labels preserve only explicit data dependencies; A21-A24 and A34-A37 remain retired. Each A row's literal prestate, sole request, success schema, permitted delta, hard-stop condition, immediate/final entity readback, and affected P rows are controlling. The A02 entity endpoint remains `FrameUpdateContext` size decimal `12` (`0x0c`), `member_count:4`, with exactly `callbackFrame@0x0/4 unsigned int`, `currentFrame@0x4/4 unsigned int`, `dispatching@0x8/1 bool`, and `_idaTailPadding09@0x9/3 unsigned char[3]`; the rejected size-`0x0a`/three-member form is never acceptable. Each rename's dry and persistent response must satisfy the authoritative row/summary allowlists above, including semantic target equality, while each anchored registry response satisfies its absent-or-null error/cursor contract and the dry run leaves its pair unchanged. A01-A02, all thirteen `set_type` rows, and all twelve `set_function_comments` rows each retain their exact sole success row under the non-rename allowlists/identity map. E01 is the sole operational prerequisite; this report does not prescribe transaction ordering, repeated global sweeps, saving, reopening, promotion, or rollback.

## First-Draft C++ Recommendation

The formal C++ below intentionally retains only the three semantic `FrameUpdateContext` fields. A02's `_idaTailPadding09` exists solely to make the current IDA parser represent the already-required `0x0c` size deterministically; it must not be copied into ordinary or generated source, where the size assertion verifies the compiler's natural tail padding.

Exact UID00005G H fragment at position 0:

```text
#ifndef NEXUSTK_UI_CORE_FRAMEMGR_H
#define NEXUSTK_UI_CORE_FRAMEMGR_H

#include "../../util/LObject.h"
#include "../../util/Singleton.h"
#include <list>

struct FrameUpdateContext
{
    unsigned int callbackFrame;
    unsigned int currentFrame;
    bool dispatching;
};

class FrameHandler
{
public:
    virtual ~FrameHandler();
    void ScheduleNextFrame();
    void RemoveFrame();

protected:
    void ScheduleFrameCallback(int delayFrames);
    virtual void OnFrameUpdate(const FrameUpdateContext *context) = 0;
};

struct FrameScheduleEntry
{
    FrameHandler *handler;
    unsigned int dueFrame;
};

class FrameMgr;

typedef char FrameUpdateContextSizeMustBe12[
    sizeof(FrameUpdateContext) == 0x0c ? 1 : -1];
typedef char FrameScheduleEntrySizeMustBe8[
    sizeof(FrameScheduleEntry) == 0x08 ? 1 : -1];
```

Exact UID0000Q0 CPP/H fragments at position 10. The CPP comment is source-neutral and prevents the validator from rendering a false empty-emitter marker; UID0002AT remains the sole definition carrier:

```text
// The declaration is emitted in FrameMgr.h; UID0002AT owns the definition.
```

```text
extern FrameMgr *g_frameRegistry;
```

Exact UID00005H H fragment at position 30:

```text
class FrameMgr : public LObject, public Singleton<FrameMgr>
{
public:
    FrameMgr();
    void DispatchDueFrameCallbacks(unsigned int currentFrame);

private:
    friend class FrameHandler;

    void Insert(FrameHandler *handler, unsigned int dueFrame);
    void RemoveFrame(FrameHandler *handler);
    void AdvanceCallbackFrame(bool dispatching);

    FrameUpdateContext m_context;
    std::list<FrameScheduleEntry> m_callbacks;
};

typedef char FrameMgrSizeMustBe24[
    sizeof(FrameMgr) == 0x18 ? 1 : -1];

#endif
```

Exact UID00005G CPP fragment at position 0:

```text
#include "FrameMgr.h"

FrameHandler::~FrameHandler()
{
    g_frameRegistry->RemoveFrame(this);
}

void FrameHandler::ScheduleNextFrame()
{
    g_frameRegistry->Insert(this, g_frameRegistry->m_context.currentFrame + 1);
}

void FrameHandler::RemoveFrame()
{
    g_frameRegistry->RemoveFrame(this);
}

void FrameHandler::ScheduleFrameCallback(int delayFrames)
{
    g_frameRegistry->Insert(
        this, g_frameRegistry->m_context.callbackFrame + delayFrames);
}
```

Exact UID00005H CPP fragment at position 30:

```text
FrameMgr::FrameMgr()
{
    m_context.dispatching = false;
    m_context.callbackFrame = 0;
    m_context.currentFrame = 0;
}

void FrameMgr::Insert(FrameHandler *handler, unsigned int dueFrame)
{
    FrameScheduleEntry entry;
    entry.handler = handler;
    entry.dueFrame = dueFrame;

    std::list<FrameScheduleEntry>::iterator position = m_callbacks.begin();
    while (position != m_callbacks.end() &&
           position->dueFrame <= dueFrame) {
        ++position;
    }
    m_callbacks.insert(position, entry);
}

void FrameMgr::RemoveFrame(FrameHandler *handler)
{
    std::list<FrameScheduleEntry>::iterator position = m_callbacks.begin();
    while (position != m_callbacks.end()) {
        if (position->handler == handler)
            position = m_callbacks.erase(position);
        else
            ++position;
    }
}

void FrameMgr::DispatchDueFrameCallbacks(unsigned int currentFrame)
{
    const unsigned int previousFrame = m_context.currentFrame;
    m_context.currentFrame = currentFrame;
    if (previousFrame == currentFrame) {
        m_context.callbackFrame = currentFrame;
        return;
    }

    m_context.dispatching = true;
    while (!m_callbacks.empty() &&
           m_callbacks.front().dueFrame < currentFrame) {
        m_context.callbackFrame = m_callbacks.front().dueFrame;
        FrameHandler *handler = m_callbacks.front().handler;
        m_callbacks.pop_front();
        handler->OnFrameUpdate(&m_context);
    }

    m_context.dispatching = false;
    while (!m_callbacks.empty() &&
           m_callbacks.front().dueFrame == currentFrame) {
        m_context.callbackFrame = m_callbacks.front().dueFrame;
        FrameHandler *handler = m_callbacks.front().handler;
        m_callbacks.pop_front();
        handler->OnFrameUpdate(&m_context);
    }
    m_context.callbackFrame = currentFrame;
}

void FrameMgr::AdvanceCallbackFrame(bool dispatching)
{
    const unsigned int currentFrame = m_context.currentFrame;
    m_context.dispatching = dispatching;
    while (!m_callbacks.empty() &&
           m_callbacks.front().dueFrame <= currentFrame) {
        FrameHandler *handler = m_callbacks.front().handler;
        m_callbacks.pop_front();
        handler->OnFrameUpdate(&m_context);
    }
    ++m_context.callbackFrame;
}
```

```text
#include "ScreenPane.h"
#include "../../util/FunctionObjects.h"

void ScreenPane::OnFrameUpdate(const FrameUpdateContext *context)
{
    if (m_fadeTransitionState == 2) {
        if (!context->dispatching || m_fadeStep == 0)
            RenderPresentation();
        if (--m_fadeStep >= 0) {
            ScheduleFrameCallback(1);
            return;
        }
    } else {
        if (m_fadeTransitionState != 3)
            return;
        if (!context->dispatching || m_fadeStep == 24)
            RenderPresentation();
        if (++m_fadeStep <= 24) {
            ScheduleFrameCallback(1);
            return;
        }
    }

    if (m_fadeCompletionCallback != 0) {
        m_fadeCompletionCallback->Invoke();
        delete m_fadeCompletionCallback;
        m_fadeCompletionCallback = 0;
    }

    const bool showCursor = m_fadePreviousValue != 0;
    m_fadeTransitionState = 1;
    if (showCursor)
        ::ShowCursor(TRUE);
}
```
